# Institutional requirements traceability register

Read-only trace of every requirement in `docs/INSTITUTIONAL_ARCHITECTURE_SPEC.md` (six parts) against the code in `app/src` on 21 October 2026 (main checkout, after step I0.1), with the plan step that delivers each one (`docs/INSTITUTIONAL_ARCHITECTURE_PLAN.md` §5). Machine-readable rows: `docs/requirements/requirements.json`. Re-run from the repository root: `python3 -I app/scripts/requirements/extract.py .` (scripts in `app/scripts/requirements/`; status knowledge base `rules.py`, step reassignment under the revised plan `reassign.py`). Checked by `app/src/lib/requirements/requirements.test.ts`.

**Binding (plan §5, Traceability).** This register is binding: a step's brief lists the requirement IDs it delivers; a step is finished only when each is met or its deviation is written down; a change that meets or breaks a requirement updates its row (edit `rules.py` and re-run).

**How rows were made.** `parse_spec.py` extracts one row per table data row, list item, numbered workflow stage, arrow-chain stage (chains in table cells too), form (by `form_id`) and each of its `decision`/`rules`/`controls` keys, each key of a non-form JSON contract, each item of an inline list (`Indicators: …`, `KPIs: …`, `Records: …`, roles/registers lists), each title/description pair in the unheaded layouts (readiness dimensions, security layers, roadmap …), each launch-checklist item, and each prose sentence that states a requirement (must / should / never / only / do not … or an imperative). Form *fields* are not split (the forms agent covers them). Lines 1–29 (provenance note) are not specification text.

**Status rules (strict).** *Met*: the behaviour exists end to end (procedure, register, permission) and, where possible, a test proves it. *Partial*: some of it exists; the gap is named. *Not yet*: nothing beyond a screen or a word. *Platform rule stricter*: a binding CLAUDE.md rule decides more strictly (cited). *Country decision*: the spec leaves it to the nation; the row says what the platform provides for it. *Lower priority*: plan §4 (still tracked, step given). The 91 acceptance rows take their status from `docs/INSTITUTIONAL_ACCEPTANCE.md` (Proven → Met). Steps: `—` nothing further for the row; I0.2–I11 and Throughout as in plan §5; `Unassigned` = no step delivers it. Rows the first plan text left without a step carry `first_plan_step: Unassigned` in the JSON. Department rows add I2, KPI rows add I3, form rows add I1 in `also_steps`.

**Self-check.** 176 numbered section headings found; sections with zero rows: none.

## Already built but short of a requirement (fix before or during the next steps)

Each item was verified in `app/src` on 21 October 2026, in the main checkout.

1. **Readiness is a generated percentage that gates activation.** `lib/institution/readiness.ts:39-131` `scoreInstitution()` builds a weighted 0-100 score from archetype constants. Its "Audit integrity" dimension is fixed at 100 and "Cascade resilience" defaults to 90. `activateMinistry()` (`lib/data/store/ministries.ts:118-122`) refuses or allows activation on that score, and `/command`, `/ministries`, OpsCenter, NationalShell and MinistryWorkspace show it. This conflicts with the spec (l.2029, l.3378, l.4035) and plan §3.4 ("never stored as a separate score"). I0.2 stops it gating activation; I2 replaces it: keep the views, but derive each dimension from evidence and show "Not assessed" until a check exists.
2. **Who acted is lost on institution-structure changes.** `lib/data/store/ministries.ts:104-177` audits create, rename, activate, deactivate, merge, department add and remove, and module set with the literal actor `'operator'`. So do `lib/data/store/federation.ts:61,441,490` (integration register, webhooks) and `lib/data/store/platform-ops.ts:57,101,216,236`. The routes check `adminOnly`, but the verified official's name never reaches the chain. This breaks CLAUDE.md "Who acted" and spec audit l.3862. Plan step I0.2.
3. **Departments are deleted, not versioned.** `removeDepartment()` filters the record out (`ministries.ts:164-169`). Departments are edited directly by an administrator, with no history and no second official. Plan step I0.2 (ended with a reason and kept), then I2.
4. **Reference procedures pre-fill statutory day counts.** See plan-weaker item 1. This breaks spec l.249 and l.1241 and CLAUDE.md "Never type a statutory … deadline into code". Fix in I1 by turning deadline and appeal periods into slots that need a cited value at approval.
5. **Training and demo can send real notifications.** `lib/messaging/` and `lib/events/deliver.ts` never consult `installationMode()`/`samplesAllowed()`. The spec requires real notifications to be blocked in simulation modes and webhooks to enforce the boundary (l.4498, l.4501). Plan step I0.2.
6. **Local government onboarding claims success it does not have.** `onboardMunicipality()` (`lib/data/store/permits.ts:158-197`) answers `status: 'provisioned'` with a go-live estimate and stores nothing. This breaks spec l.3578 ("must not claim that an external check succeeded"). Plan step I0.2.
7. **Forms cannot mark protected identities or confidentiality classes.** `FieldSpec.personal` is a boolean (`lib/casework/types.ts:21`), there is no protected-identity class for parties, and a normal-sensitivity complaint lists the complainant's name (audit C S-1, high). I1 must add these before I8's complaint and disclosure procedures can exist.
8. **The event outbox broadcasts too much.** CloudEvents carry the actor name and free-text detail to every subscriber, from a fixed source `/civicos/platform`, signed with HMAC only (audit C F-1). This breaks spec l.3736 ("only the information required"). It is a live privacy exposure as well as an I9 item.
9. **Exchange services are bound to integrations, not to relationships.** Ending a relationship between institutions does not stop `callService()` (acceptance P5-05). This breaks spec l.3653 and l.4045 and belongs to I9.
10. **Keys and releases.** There is one installation-wide data key and one set of signing keys (`lib/keys/custody.ts`), so independent institutions share the operator's keys (spec l.3887). Release DSSE signatures are placeholders and digests are synthetic (`lib/data/supply-chain.ts:59-109`; spec l.4055). Both are named in the revised I9.
11. **Directives are not law-cited records.** `lib/data/directives.ts` stores `citation` as free text, not a law-register `Citation`, and has no required response or end of effective period (spec §3.4 l.228). Fix with I5.
12. **No collective decision step.** Case packages have no vote or quorum step type, so Cabinet (spec l.243) and a central-bank board (l.693-695) cannot be modelled; only the legislature has majorities. Add it when I5 adds the Cabinet decision. The platform minimum of two officials still applies.
13. **Audit entries hide institution and purpose in free text.** `AuditEntry` has no institution, purpose or correlation field (audit C A-1), so an institution cannot be shown "its own audit history" (spec l.2851). Adding them needs a new hashed entry format; CLAUDE.md forbids changing the existing field list in place.
14. **Navigation ignores permissions.** `lib/nav/console-nav.ts` shows every item to every official. The backend enforces access, but the spec wants navigation to reflect permissions (l.4222). This belongs to I10.
15. **Immigration has no fee.** The residence-permit workflow names "applicable fees" (l.1503), but immigration procedures issue no bill from an approved tariff; only tax and customs use `issueBill()`.
16. **Health inspections are not structured records.** On licence cases an inspection is a document that any officer of the regulator can attach (`lib/organisations/procedures.ts:219-252`). The spec wants findings entered under an assigned inspector's account (l.1336-1337). This is I4.
